<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, May 8, 2018
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, May 8, 2018, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to 5
minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any comments
but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be placed on a
future agenda.
 
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature and
will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be removed
from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: April 24, 2018  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000, April 2018  
 
c. Constable Reports: Winslow Precinct #2, March and Kayenta Precinct #4, April 2018  
 
d. Personnel Actions  
 
e. Reissue Stale Dated Checks  
 
f. Amendment No. 3 to Contract #B15-04-027  for Hauling Waste Tires extending the contract one
additional year to June 8, 2019
 
 
g. Amendment No. 1 to Contract #B17-05-022 Pavement Markings, extending the contract for one
additional year with no cost increase
 
 
h. Application for supervised fireworks display permit and permit to shoot an anvil submitted by
the Heber-Overgaard Chamber of Commerce 
 
 
i. Chief Elected Officials (CEO) and Local Workforce Development Board (LWDB) Partnership

<!-- page 2 -->
i. Chief Elected Officials (CEO) and Local Workforce Development Board (LWDB) Partnership
Agreement for the Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Board (NEAZ LWDB),
as revised on April 19, 2018
 
 
j. Tax exemptions filed pursuant to ARS §42-11153 as a Request for Redemption of Waiver  
 
k. Contracts signed by County Manager per Board of Supervisors Resolution: Professional
Services Agreement with Painted Sky Engineering & Survey, LLC for Lone Pine Dam Road
Signing and Striping Plans
 
 
l. Settlement of Notice of Claim  
 
m. Resolution Number 6-18, approving the Small Claims Settlement Process  
 
3. SUPERINTENDENT OF SCHOOLS:  
 
a. Presentation to Jett Jue, 8th grade student from Winslow USD, as 3 time Navajo County Spelling
Bee Champion.
 
Presented By: Jalyn Gerlich
 
4. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Presentation by Highland Economics on Adverse Effects on Coal Communities.  
Presented By: Paul Watson
 
b. Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 07-18, opposing the
"NextGen Climate Action" ballot initiative. 
 
Presented By: Rod Ross, Arizona Public Service 
 
5. ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
 
a. Presentation on the current fire season including entering into Stage 2 Fire Restrictions and
Tinder Fire information.
 
Presented By: Jeff Lee
 
6. TREASURER:  
 
a. Consideration and approval of the Abatement of Personal Property Taxes.  
Presented By: Debra Kester

<!-- page 3 -->
7. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
1. Intergovernmental Agreement (IGA) Amendment for the 2019 Fiscal Year between the
Arizona Department of Health Services and the Navajo County Public Health Services
District Tobacco and Chronic Disease Program
 
 
8. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration of approving the cancelation of contract #B17-07-028 with J. Banicki
Construction, Inc. for repairs on Woodruff Concrete Bridge.
 
Presented By: Bill Bess
 
b. Consideration of Award of Contract #B18-03-014 Supplying and Delivering Liquid Asphalt
Products to Cactus Transport, Inc. 
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
c. Consideration of Award of Contract #B18-03-007 Supply & Delivery of Culverts and Bands
to Pacific Ponderosa Co.  
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
d. Consideration of Approving Task Agreement No. 6 to Memorandum of Agreement between
Hopi Department of Transportation and Navajo County for the purchasing of aggregate
material on Hopi public roads.  
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
e. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
a. Consideration of approving a $5,000 funding request for the Little Colorado River Plateau
RC&D to support weed control/management in Rainbow Lake for tourism, recreation, fishing,
swimming and boating.
 
Presented By: Sandra Phillips
 
f. VICTORY HEIGHTS ROAD MAINTENANCE DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
a. Amendment No. 1 to the Victory Heights Road Maintenance District Contract, Contract
#B17-05-016, for road maintenance and related improvements, extending the contract for one
additional year to July 10, 2019 with no cost increase

<!-- page 4 -->
9. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and
Board members.
 
 
 
10. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: May 3, 2018 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 3:37 p.m. By: MB                          
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.e.        
Meeting Date: 05/08/2018  
Title: Reissuance of Stale Dated Checks
Submitted For: Debra Kester, Treasurer  Submitted By: Cindy Miller, Accountant
Department: Treasurer
Motion before the Board:
Reissue Stale Dated Checks
Background:
 
Background: 
 
Treasurer’s checks were issued as refunds for overpaid property taxes or for investor payments.
The checks were voided because they had been outstanding for a year or more.  The payees were
contacted and are requesting replacement checks.  All have completed the required Affidavit of
Duplicate Warrant form.  Upon Board approval, new checks will be issued to the following
individuals pursuant to ARS 11-632.
 
Issue Date                 Check Number              Amount                  Name on Check
 
8/11/16                       1300016777                   185.10                  Richard Westergard
 
9/22/16                       1300017001                 4,655.00                 Rpt Roth 401K(Trustee Richard
Root)
 
 
 
Form Review
Inbox Reviewed By Date
Treasurer Debra Kester 04/27/2018 08:37 AM

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.f.        
Meeting Date: 05/08/2018  
Title: Approval of Amendment No. 3 to the Contract for Hauling Waste Tires
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Amendment No. 3 to Contract #B15-04-027  for Hauling Waste Tires extending the
contract one additional year to June 8, 2019
Background:

(WIOA).  The CEO and LWDB Partnership Agreement is required by the Workforce Arizona

<!-- page 473 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 2.m.        
Meeting Date: 05/08/2018  
Title: Approval of Small Claims Settlement Resolution 
Submitted By: Eric Scott, HR Director
Department: Administrative Services Division: Human Resources
Motion before the Board:
Resolution Number 6-18, approving the Small Claims Settlement Process 
Background:
The current process to settle small claims is to request approval from the Board. The process takes
time to investigate and determine fault, and once fault is determined, it is in the organization's best
interest to settle quickly. The process of getting approval from the Board to pay a claim can take
precious time, and could ultimately cost the organization more in the long run.
Navajo County Risk Management is requesting approval of this resolution which would grant the
County Manager, or his designee, the authority to settle and pay claims up to, and including, the
amount of $10,000. Any claim above $10,000 would still need to go before the Board for
approval.
*Note - Our liability deductible through ACIP (Arizona Counties Insurance Pool) is $50,000.
Attachments
Small Claims Resolution

RESOLUTION ____-18 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS, FOR THE 
ADOPTION OF SMALL CLAIMS SETTLEMENT PROCESS 
 
WHEREAS Navajo County receives a Notice of Claim; and 
 
WHEREAS the County Manager and/or designee conducts a proper investigation on the alleged 
claim of liability; and  
 
WHEREAS the County Manager and/or their designee determine that Navajo County is at fault ; and  
 
WHEREAS the Navajo County Board of Supervisors recognizes the advantage in expediting the 
settlement process.   
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County hereby 
supports the action to adopt the proposed small claims settlement process, and hereby grants the 
County Manager, or the County Manager’s designee, the authority to settle a Notice of Claim up 
to, and including, the amount of $10,000, contingent upon a completion of an internal investigatio n 
and determination of fault.  
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors on this 
___ day of ___________, 2018.

Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
  
 
 
RESOLUTION ____-18 
 
RESOLUTION OPPOSING THE “NEXTGEN CLIMATE ACTION” BALLOT 
INITIATIVE THAT WOULD REQUIRE ARIZONA UTILITY COMPANIES TO 
OBTAIN 50% OF THEIR ENERGY FROM RENEWABLE SOURCES BY 2030 
 
Whereas, an out-of-state group, “NextGen Climate Action”, funded by California billionaire 
Tom Steyer has filed a ballot initiative with the Secretary of State’s office that would amend 
Arizona’s State Constitution to require that Arizona utility companies obtain 50% of their energy 
from renewable sources by 2030, less than 12 years from now; and  
 
Whereas, if passed, the ‘Clean Energy for a Healthy Arizona’ initiative would circumvent 
Arizona’s current structure for regulating utilities, which has provided Arizona residents with 
some of the safest, most reliable and affordable energy in the country for over 100 years; and  
 
Whereas, the initiative would enshrine in the state constitution a regulatory mandate that will 
dramatically increase electricity bills, especially those of low-income and fixed-income 
residents; and 
 
Whereas, this constitutional mandate could not be changed or modified without a costly ballot 
initiative; and 
 
Whereas, the initiative would force Arizona residents to pay hundreds of millions of dollars for 
the construction of new infrastructure, including solar facilities and carbon-emitting natural gas 
plants that would be necessary to generate electricity when the sun is not shining, and wind is not 
blowing; and  
 
Whereas, the initiative includes a requirement that 20% of utilities’ renewable generation come 
from rooftop solar, which is the least efficient, most expensive form of solar; and 
 
Whereas, the initiative would require Arizona utilities to shut down power plants that have been 
providing safe, affordable and reliable energy for decades, costing taxpayers tens of millions of 
dollars in lost tax revenue and hundreds of high-paying jobs ; and 
 
Whereas, the initiative would threaten the viability of the Palo Verde nuclear generating station, 
the largest source of clean, carbon-free energy in the country, costing 3000 Arizona families their 
jobs and eliminating tens of millions of dollars of tax revenue for Arizona schools and public 
safety; and

<!-- page 479 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
Whereas, the initiative would require the closure of the Cholla and Springerville power plants, 
leading to the loss of hundreds of local jobs and millions of dollars of essential tax revenue for 
local schools and public safety; and 
 
Whereas, the initiative will have a particularly devastating impact on the state’s electric 
cooperatives, which do not have the financial capacity to absorb the costs that would result from 
this 50% renewable energy mandate; and 
 
Whereas, the electric cooperatives provide an essential and irreplaceable public service for 
Arizona’s rural residents, supplying life-preserving affordable and reliable power for hundreds of 
thousands of rural families; and  
 
Whereas, an independent analysis by the Seidman Research Institute at Arizona State 
University’s W.P. Carey School of Business finds that the “Clean Energy for a Healthy 
Arizona” ballot proposal would have devastating immediate and long-term impacts to the 
Arizona economy, including the loss of 547,000 job years, $72.5 billion in Gross State Product, 
and $42.5 billion in disposable personal income; and 
 
Whereas, the study’s lead author, Dr. Timothy James, concluded that, “this ballot initiative 
would have a significant dampening effect on the Arizona economy… in lost jobs, reduced 
personal incomes and diminished economic productivity.”  
 
Therefore, be it resolved by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, that the members of the 
Board oppose the “NextGen Climate Action” ballot initiative and urge County residents to vote 
no on the initiative if it is on the ballot in November. 
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors on this 
8th day of May, 2018.  
 
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
 By ________________________________________ 
                                                                            Steve Williams, Chairman of the Board 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 5.a.        
Meeting Date: 05/08/2018  
Title: 2018 Fire Season Update
Submitted For: Catrina Jenkins, EMP Director 
Submitted By: Catrina Jenkins, EMP Director
Department: Administrative Services Division: Emergency Management
Presented By: Jeff Lee
Subject:
Presentation on the current fire season including entering into Stage 2 Fire Restrictions and Tinder
Fire information.
Background: 
The partners of the White Mountain Fire Restrictions Working Group including Navajo County
have unanimously decided to enter into Stage 2 Fire Restrictions as of May 4 at 12:01 a.m.  
STAGE II – Moderate Restrictions
The following acts are prohibited until further notice: 
Building maintaining, attending, or using a fire, campfire, charcoal, coal, or wood stove
including fires in developed campgrounds or improved sites.
1.
Smoking, except within an enclosed vehicle or building.2.
Outdoor Mechanical and Industrial Prohibitions3.
Operating any internal combustion engine in the course of mechanical or industrial
operations that would produce open flames or sparks.
a.
Welding, or operating acetylene or other torch with open flame.b.
Using an explosive.c.
Operating motorized vehicles off designated roads and trails.4.
Use of any and all fireworks.1.
Use of explosive targets.2.
Use of tracer round ammunition.3.
 
Exemptions: 
Persons with a written permit that specifically authorizes the otherwise prohibited act.1.
Industrial operations where specific operations and exemptions are identified and mitigation
measures are implemented as outlined in an agency plan.
2.
Persons operating internal combustion engines with spark arrestors such as lawnmowers
and landscaping equipment in maintained landscaped space.
3.

<!-- page 481 -->
Welding, or operating acetylene or other torch with open flame in an enclosed or developed
area designated for that purpose that is equipped with appropriate fire protection. 
4.
Persons using a device fueled solely by liquid petroleum or LPG fuels that can be turned on
and off. Such devices can only be used in an area that is barren or cleared of all overhead
and surrounding flammable materials within 3 feet of the device.
5.
Operating generators with an approved spark arresting device within an enclosed vehicle or
building or in an area that is barren or cleared of all overhead and surrounding flammable
materials within three feet of the generator.
6.
Operating motorized vehicles on designated roads and trails so long as you park in an area
devoid of vegetation within 10 feet of the roadway.
7.
Emergency repair of public utilities and railroads and mitigation measures are implemented
as outlined in an agency plan.
8.
Persons conducting activities in those designated areas where the activity is specifically
authorized by written posted notice.
9.
Any Federal, State, or local officer, or member of an organized rescue or firefighting force
in the performance of an official duty.
10.
All land within a city boundary is exempted unless otherwise stated by city ordinance.11.
An exemption does not absolve an individual or organization from liability or responsibility for
any fire started by the exempted activity.

Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Tinder Fire 2018
Navajo County Impacts

<!-- page 483 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Winslow
• Many thanks to our partners in 
Winslow for stepping up to the 
plate and being willing to help 
our neighbors from Happy Jack
• Winslow Unified School 
District opened the doors to 
the Junior High Gym to use 
as a shelter
• City of Winslow personnel 
were on hand and willing to 
assist in any way possible.

<!-- page 484 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Smoke impacts
• Arizona Department of Environmental 
Quality has provided portable air 
particulate monitors in both Winslow 
and Holbrook
• Navajo County Public Health and 
Navajo County Emergency 
Management have been monitoring 
these to watch for “unhealthy” levels 
of smoke impacting areas in Navajo 
County
• A press release was issued to inform 
the public of what to do to mitigate 
the impacts of the smoke.

<!-- page 485 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Partnerships
• Jeff Lineberry deployed to the fire 
with the TOAD 4 communications 
van to help with connections to the 
Coconino Emergency Operations 
Center
• Navajo County Sheriff’s Office 
personnel also responded to assist 
with evacuations
• Navajo County Fairgrounds was open 
to large and small animals for 
sheltering

Meeting Date: 05/08/2018  
Title: IGA Tobacco and Chronic Disease Program
Submitted For: Jeffrey Lee, Health Director  Submitted By: Darcey McKee, Program
Manager
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Agreement (IGA) Amendment for the 2019 Fiscal Year between the Arizona
Department of Health Services and the Navajo County Public Health Services District Tobacco
and Chronic Disease Program
Background:
The tobacco program has been funded through the Arizona Department of Health Services since
1997, to provide residents with information and education on the dangers of tobacco
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2019
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested: 180,000.00
Fiscal Impact:
IGA funds received from the Arizona Department of Health Services will support the on-going
work of the tobacco and chronic disease program.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.a.        
Meeting Date: 05/08/2018  
Title: Cancellation of Contract # B17-07-028: Woodruff Concrete Bridge Repairs
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Bryan Cook, Sr. Engineering Project Manager II
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Bill Bess
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of approving the cancelation of contract #B17-07-028 with J. Banicki Construction,
Inc. for repairs on Woodruff Concrete Bridge.
Background:
Navajo County staff and Jerry Cannon, the Engineer of Design and Record, have determined that
J. Banicki Construction, Inc. (Banicki), the contract holder for the repairs on Woodruff Concrete
Bridge, has not provided evidence of the necessary experience required by the Special Provisions
provided in contract # B17-07-028-SP2.0(c). The following requirement is stipulated in the
contract solicitation:  2.0(c) Contactor  Qualifications. The Contractor shall submit
documentation of successful projects placing structural concrete (bridge decks or concrete
pavement) and PPC overlay systems to establish grade lines using similar equipment as specified
herein within the past  five years. The project experience documentation the company
provided failed to prove experience with placing the specialized material used for the bridge deck
repair as required. 
Specifically, Banicki listed a joint venture bridge project that cited use of polyester polymer
concrete (PPC) material, but under closer investigation and calling the listed reference, it was
discovered that Banicki's role in the project involved jacking of the bridge, but not the placement
of the PPC material as required.  Before concluding that Banicki had not provided evidence of
experience placing the material, the Navajo County team conducted a conference call with
Banicki to address their experience submittal and to allow Banicki the opportunity to re-address
our concerns of inexperience with PPC. When the company did not provide any further examples
of projects where they placed the material, the determination was made that Banicki lacked the
sufficient experience required by contract to complete the work.
Staff has determined that canceling the contract and re-bidding is in the best interests of Navajo
County. Upon cancellation of contract, Navajo County Public Works will return the
contractor's Bid Bond.  Navajo County Public Works will re-bid this project to receive a bidder
who meets the experience required under 2.0(c) as well as all other contract requirements. 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends cancellation of contract # B17-07-028 for repairs on Woodruff Concrete Bridge

<!-- page 508 -->
Staff recommends cancellation of contract # B17-07-028 for repairs on Woodruff Concrete Bridge
with J. Banicki Construction, Inc.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.b.

Title: Possible Award Contract #B18-03-014 Supplying and Delivering Liquid Asphalt
Products 
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Cheryl Hollins, Account Specialist
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Award of Contract #B18-03-014 Supplying and Delivering Liquid Asphalt
Products to Cactus Transport, Inc. 
Background:
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Governmental
Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m. M.S.T., April 25, 2018 in strict accordance with the
specifications on file in the office of the Navajo County Department of Public Works.  No bids
were accepted after 3:00 p.m. M.S.T.  The bids were publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15 p.m.
 
This is an eighteen-month contract to provide the sale and delivery (as required), the application,
and other aspects of liquid asphalt products on public roads in Navajo County.  A total of two bids
were received, of which, one was rejected (Hawker & Evans Asphalt Co., Inc.) due to the altering
of the Bid Schedule Table 3.  The product specified by Hawker & Evans Asphalt Co., Inc. was not
approved by the County Engineer prior to the bid opening.  Staff is recommending award of
contract to Cactus Transport, Inc.
 
 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2018
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
Funds are budgeted in both Fiscal Years 2018 and 2019.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.d.        
Meeting Date: 05/08/2018  
Title: Hopi Tribe BIA 43 Gravel Placement
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Miles Begay, Engineering Tribal Trans Manager
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Approving Task Agreement No. 6 to Memorandum of Agreement between Hopi
Department of Transportation and Navajo County for the purchasing of aggregate material on
Hopi public roads.  
Background:
The Hopi Division of Transportation (HDOT) was recently established by the Hopi Tribe within
the past 5 years and are currently operating from a small building, with plans to expand their
operation. They have identified some property near the Hopi Landfill that they plan to use to
build their own HDOT road yard which will become their main operations location. BIA Route
43 is a stretch of dirt road that has low water crossings and material made of clay that can cause
difficulties during inclement weather. It is the primary access route for accessing the proposed
road yard and future Hopi facilities.  It also serves as the main route for commuters traveling
between the Hardrock and Pinon communities, and for heavy sanitation vehicles utilizing the
landfill. With all the traffic that utilizes this route and future developments that are bound to occur
near this route, the HDOT Director recognizes that BIA 43 requires attention for graveling to keep
the road passable. Navajo County plans to purchase and deliver approximately 760 cubic yards of
aggregate material to BIA Route 43; expenditures are not to exceed $45,000. HDOT does plan to
partner with the County by providing labor to apply the material on BIA Route 43.  
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends that the Board of Supervisors approve the attached Task Agreement between
Hopi DOT and Navajo County.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2018
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $45,000
Fiscal Impact:
District ll Hopi CIP Funds-$20,000
District ll Special Road Funds- $25,000

<!-- page 521 -->
Fiscal Year: 2018
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
Expenditures not to exceed the combined amount of $20,000 from District ll Hopi CIP Funds and
$25,000 from the District ll Special Road Funds.

regular meeting on May 8 , 201 8, has approved the expenditure of up to

Highway User Revenue Funds (check appropriate box) (“Allocate d Funds”) to be 
used for the purchase of ___Aggregate Material _,  for road maintenance of 
Hopi Tribe’s  public roads which is located on the Hopi Indian Reservation , in 
Navajo County, within Supervisorial District No. 2 of the County.  The Allocated 
Funds shall be used to supplement other monies that are or may become 
available to the Tribe for road maintenance purposes on Hopi Tribe’s  public 
roads in that portion of the Hopi Indian Reservation  within Navajo County, 
Supervisorial District No. 2 of the County.  The Tribe understands and 
acknowledges that the Allocated Funds are monies that the County has received 
from the Arizona Highway User Rev enue Fund (HURF) and which are subject to 
audit by the Arizona Auditor General. 
 
The Tribe warrants and represents that the _Aggregate Material __ purchased 
with the Allocated Funds will be used primarily for maintenance activities on 
roads within District 2, which are open to the public and are eligible for the 
expenditure of HURF monies under the Arizona Constitution and related Arizona 
Revised Statutes.  The BIA agrees to fully cooperate in any audit of the Allocated 
Funds by the Arizona Auditor General. 
 
The receipt of the Allocated Funds shall not cause other monies to be diverted 
from projects within this area or allocated to projects outside this area. 
 
For any material acquired in whole or in part with the Allocated Funds, the 
acquiring party shall be  solely responsible for all expenses  of storing and 
applying the material .  The County shall have no responsibility or liability 
whatsoever in regard to such material. The Tribe shall be responsible for 
identifying, prioritizing, scheduling, and performing  all maintenance activities for 
which the Allocated Funds are used in whole or in part.  It is understood that the 
County’s sole obligation hereunder is to make payment of the Allocated Funds. 
 
Any Allocated Funds that are unused as of June 30, 20 18, shall no longer be 
available for distribution pursuant to this Task Agreement. 
 
The Allocated Funds under this agreement shall be paid by Navajo County 
directly to the invoicing party upon the County’s receipt of invoices or billing 
statement that show that the  items have been purchased and approved by the 
Tribe.  The total of all invoices shall not exceed the approved amount from this 
agreement.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.e.1.a.        
Meeting Date: 05/08/2018  
Title: Little Colorado River Plateau RC&D funding request to support Rainbow Lake
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Sandra Phillips, Assistant County Engineer/Flood Control Manager
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Sandra Phillips
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of approving a $5,000 funding request for the Little Colorado River Plateau RC&D
to support weed control/management in Rainbow Lake for tourism, recreation, fishing, swimming
and boating.
Background:
Navajo County has been an active partner with the Rainbow Lake Coalition in the maintenance
effort to remove noxious aquatic weeds from Rainbow Lake.  In past years Navajo County has
contributed $5,000 annually to assist the Rainbow Lake Coalition in their efforts to control the
weeds by administering herbicide treatments and with Amur Carp.
The preferred alternative in removing aquatic weeds is to introduce the Amur Carp; a sterile fish
which can eat three-quarters of its weight in weeds every day and has a long life span.  This is the
most environmentally safe and cost effective solution.  The Arizona Game & Fish Department has
agreed to the use of the Amur Carp with the installation of a barrier design to contain the fish to
Rainbow Lake.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.

Board of Supervisors Regular 8.f.1.a.        
Meeting Date: 05/08/2018  
Title: B17-05-016 Victory Heights Road Maintenance District Amendment No. 1
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Amendment No. 1 to the Victory Heights Road Maintenance District Contract, Contract
#B17-05-016, for road maintenance and related improvements, extending the contract for one
additional year to July 10, 2019 with no cost increase
Background:
On August 21, 2006, the Victory Heights Road Maintenance District was formed by Resolution
#68-06.  On July 11, 2017 the Board of Directors approved a contract with Perkins Cinders, Inc.
for road maintenance and related improvements.  This Contract may be renewed for up to four
additional one-year terms.  This contract has worked very well for this District.  Therefore, we are
recommending that the contract be extended one additional year (July 11, 2018 to July 10, 2019)
with no cost increase.
 
Concurrence has been received by the Victory Heights Road Maintenance District Advisory
Committee and the Contractor.
 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2018
Budgeted Y/N: Y

Fiscal Impact:
Costs associated with this contract are through assessments to the Road Maintenance District.
